0|will be used to react to triggered
1|abilities or spells. Reactions are super
4|fast and can lead to lots of exciting
6|interactions as they resolve before all
8|other spells. For example, if you have a
11|stupify in your hand and your opponent
13|activates a spell that really bothers
15|you, you can use stupify as a reaction
17|and draw a card before your opponent's
19|spell is resolved. This might allow you
22|to draw another reaction that saves you
23|from the situation, like a defy. Another
27|exciting example is dealing damage to a
29|unit with a spell before combat. If you
32|go into combat with a unit that's
33|already damaged, you should make sure
35|you win the fight. Otherwise, the units
37|will heal after the combat. Let's say
40|you have dealt six damage to your
42|opponent's mind spplitter with falling
43|star and you go into combat against it
45|with your watchful sentry. Your opponent
47|might want to activate an action in
48|response such as grim resolve to
51|generate extra might and win the combat.
53|You can still respond to that with a
55|stupify. You reduce the mind spplitter's
57|smite to six and because it still has
59|six damage counters on it, it will die
61|before the grim resolve can save it.

16799|Welcome to Riftbound Basics featuring
16802|actions and reactions. There are three
16804|different types of spells in Riftbounds.
16806|Normal spells, which you can use to
16808|start a chain during your own turn from
16809|a neutral game state. Actions, which can
16811|be used in exactly the same way, but can
16814|also be used in combat to start a chain,
16816|and reactions, which have all the
16818|advantages of the other two, but can
16820|also be used to react to triggered
16822|abilities or spells. Reactions are super
16825|fast and can lead to lots of exciting
16826|interactions as they resolve before all
16829|other spells. For example, if you have a
16832|stupify in your hand and your opponent
16833|activates a spell that really bothers
16835|you, you can use stupify as a reaction
16838|and draw a card before your opponent's
16840|spell is resolved. This might allow you
16842|to draw another reaction that saves you
16844|from their situation, like a defy.
16847|Another exciting example is dealing
16849|damage to a unit with a spell before
16851|combat. If you go into combat with a
16853|unit that's already damaged, you should
16855|make sure you win the fight. Otherwise,
16857|the units will heal after the combat.
16860|Let's say you have dealt six damage to
16862|your opponent's mind splitter with
16863|falling star and you go into combat
16865|against it with your watchful sentry.
16867|Your opponent might want to activate an
16868|action in response, such as grim resolve
16871|to generate extra might and win the
16872|combat. You can still respond to that
16875|with a stupify. You reduced the mind
16877|spplitter's smite to six. And because it
16879|still has six damage counters on it, it
16881|will die before the grim resolve can
16883|save it. Which one is your favorite
16885|reaction card in Rift Bond? Let us know.
